Chain: analytic_v8.py. Engine with the chain's rules: the same engine with the daily loss limit, minimum and profitable days and the daily flat switched off, the concentration caps applied per trade, and payouts only at the payout target; it stays flat at weekends, because a position held through a weekend gaps and a gap is not in the chain. 24 independent two-year paths × 500 attempts, intervals cluster-robust over paths (Student t, 23 degrees of freedom). Engine with all rules: the final run. The middle columns test the implementation; the gap between them and the last columns is what the daily rules do.

Implementation. With the chain's rules, the engine's pass rate is within its 95% interval of the chain's in 23 of 24 programmes and its funded cash in 20 of 24; the largest standardised differences are -1.3 and -1.4 intervals. Remaining differences come from Monte Carlo noise and from what the engine still does that the chain does not: it closes positions at the Friday close (a fair outcome between stop and target, which the binary chain does not have), and the chain is solved on a grid. They go both ways, so neither is 'the conservative one'.

What the daily rules do. Switching the daily rules back on changes the value of an attempt by −$339 to +$462 across programmes. These are the effects the formulas of Part I do not contain; every reported figure comes from the engine with every rule on.

Part II

Every programme in the plan

One chapter per firm, for the programme and market the plan uses there: the rules as published; exactly what the engine simulates; the search; the chosen setting and its alternatives on fresh paths; one trade in dollars and points; the evaluation and the funded account from the exact chain; value per attempt and per month; sizes and number of accounts; higher costs; and how the plan meets the rules.

12.5 One trade

Risk \(l = \$1{,}500\); stop \(s = m\sigma = 0.75 \times 0.2737\% = 0.2053\%\) of the price; at 31,070.00 that is 63.8 points.

Notional \(N = l/s = \$730{,}758\); \(\$23.52\) per point; lots = this divided by the contract's dollars per point per lot, rounded down.

Cost \(c = \kappa N = \$46.77\), \(\rho = c/l = 3.12\%\); the risk is sized so that \(l(1+\rho)\) fits the room (5.4).

First trade of phase 1: \(w = \min(k\,l,\ A_1,\ \text{caps}) = \$4{,}000\); take-profit \(172.1\) points away; \(p = l/(l+w+c) = 0.2704\).

Mean \(-c\); variance \(l(w+c) = 6{,}070{,}153\); duration about 1.52 h of open market (losers 1.20 h, winners 2.4 h), cut at 20:00 UTC.

A full funded win, with every cap the engine applies (the plan's 40%, the firm's best-day share, day cap or ceiling): \(\$8{,}000\) at risk \(\$1{,}500\): take-profit 342 points away, \(p = 0.1571\).

Margin at the funded leverage 1:50: \(\$14{,}615\) = 14.6% of the starting balance (limit 60%); smallest stop \(m_{\min} = 0.183\).

12.6 The evaluation

Phase 1: \(A = \$10{,}000\), \(B = \$10{,}000\)

Chain (5.5): \(P_1 = 0.4575\), \(\mathbb{E}[\text{trades}] = 17.81\), \(K = \$793\), \(\mathbb{E}[X_\text{end}\mid\text{fail}] = -\$10{,}000\); identity gap +0.0000; textbook \((B-K)/(A+B) = 0.4599\); zero cost, exact landing \(B/(A+B) = 0.5000\).

Rules in play: every win at most $4,000; 4 trading days (filler trades in the engine).

Daily loss limit $5,000: each trade's risk plus cost fits today's room less $200; after 3 full losses in one day the remaining room is smaller than a full trade, so the plan trades smaller or waits until tomorrow.

Phase 2: \(A = \$5{,}000\), \(B = \$10{,}000\)

Chain (5.5): \(P_2 = 0.5972\), \(\mathbb{E}[\text{trades}] = 18.76\), \(K = \$846\), \(\mathbb{E}[X_\text{end}\mid\text{fail}] = -\$10{,}000\); identity gap -0.0000; textbook \((B-K)/(A+B) = 0.6094\); zero cost, exact landing \(B/(A+B) = 0.6667\).

Rules in play: every win at most $2,000; 4 trading days (filler trades in the engine).

Daily loss limit $5,000: each trade's risk plus cost fits today's room less $200; after 3 full losses in one day the remaining room is smaller than a full trade, so the plan trades smaller or waits until tomorrow.

Both phases: \(P = 0.4575 \times 0.5972 = 0.2732\) (chain). Engine with every rule, fresh paths: 0.2730 ± 0.0056.

12.8 Value per attempt and per month

12.11 How the plan meets the rules

  • Nasdaq 100 CFD, one position per account, shared direction with every other Nasdaq account
  • 1.5% risk (inside FTMO's stated 1–1.5% guidance) with the stop attached at entry
  • minimum days filled with one minimal trade per day after the target, where needed
  • every account traded the same way; none abandoned or sacrificed (no account rolling)
  • flat every day by 20:00 UTC (no entry from 19:00 UTC), so the account is flat through every daily reset and pays no overnight financing; flat at weekends
  • concentration policy: no trade and no day makes more than 40% of the current phase or payout target, so every phase takes at least three winning days
  • at least 10 minutes between a close and the next entry; no take-profit and no stop closer than 0.6 hourly standard deviations to the entry, so fewer than 1 trade in 1,000 can close within 2 minutes
  • risk per trade sized so that the stop loss plus the round-trip cost fits the room under the floor and under the day's limit; when the room above the floor is below a tenth of the normal risk, one last trade with its stop at the floor: the account recovers or ends in a breach that frees its allocation
  • minimum trading days completed, where fillers are allowed, with one minimal trade a day held at least 2 minutes and a stop of at most $3 per $100K

Sources: ftmo.com/en/faq/how-do-i-withdraw-my-profits/ (first reward 14 days after the first trade); ftmo.com/faq/how-many-accounts-can-i-have/ ($400,000 per trader); https://ftmo.com/en/blog/how-much-should-you-risk-on-one-trade/; ftmo.com (trading objectives, forbidden practices).

13.5 One trade

Risk \(l = \$1{,}750\); stop \(s = m\sigma = 1.25 \times 0.1193\% = 0.1491\%\) of the price; at 157.83 that is 23.5 pips.

Notional \(N = l/s = \$1{,}173{,}426\); \(\$74.35\) per pip; at $6.34 per pip per standard lot, 11.73 lots (round down).

Cost \(c = \kappa N = \$59.84\), \(\rho = c/l = 3.42\%\); the risk is sized so that \(l(1+\rho)\) fits the room (5.4).

First trade of phase 1: \(w = \min(k\,l,\ A_1,\ \text{caps}) = \$3{,}333\); take-profit \(45.6\) pips away; \(p = l/(l+w+c) = 0.3403\).

Mean \(-c\); variance \(l(w+c) = 5{,}938{,}062\); duration about 3.03 h of open market (losers 2.54 h, winners 4.0 h), cut at 20:00 UTC.

A full funded win, with every cap the engine applies (the plan's 40%, the firm's best-day share, day cap or ceiling): \(\$8{,}000\) at risk \(\$1{,}750\): take-profit 108 pips away, \(p = 0.1784\).

Margin at the funded leverage 1:100: \(\$11{,}734\) = 11.7% of the starting balance (limit 60%); smallest stop \(m_{\min} = 0.244\).

13.6 The evaluation

Phase 1: \(A = \$10{,}000\), \(B = \$12{,}000\)

Chain (5.5): \(P_1 = 0.4974\), \(\mathbb{E}[\text{trades}] = 18.05\), \(K = \$1{,}035\), \(\mathbb{E}[X_\text{end}\mid\text{fail}] = -\$12{,}000\); identity gap -0.0000; textbook \((B-K)/(A+B) = 0.4984\); zero cost, exact landing \(B/(A+B) = 0.5455\).

Rules in play: every win at most $4,000; 3 profitable days needed: the engine aims at a daily target (6.4), which the chain leaves out.

Daily loss limit $4,000: each trade's risk plus cost fits today's room less $200; after 2 full losses in one day the remaining room is smaller than a full trade, so the plan trades smaller or waits until tomorrow.

Phase 2: \(A = \$8{,}000\), \(B = \$12{,}000\)

Chain (5.5): \(P_2 = 0.5443\), \(\mathbb{E}[\text{trades}] = 18.38\), \(K = \$1{,}055\), \(\mathbb{E}[X_\text{end}\mid\text{fail}] = -\$12{,}000\); identity gap -0.0000; textbook \((B-K)/(A+B) = 0.5473\); zero cost, exact landing \(B/(A+B) = 0.6000\).

Rules in play: every win at most $3,200; 3 profitable days needed: the engine aims at a daily target (6.4), which the chain leaves out.

Daily loss limit $4,000: each trade's risk plus cost fits today's room less $200; after 2 full losses in one day the remaining room is smaller than a full trade, so the plan trades smaller or waits until tomorrow.

Chapter 15 · tier A

FXIFY Two Phase Classic

Nasdaq 100 CFD (US100); plan setting: risk 1.75%, \(k = 12\), \(m = 1.07\), payout target 15% of the account; $2,097 a month per 100K (± $159); in the plan: 1 × 100K; 1 × 50K; 1 × 25K; 1 × 10K; 1 × 5K, $3,734 a month.

15.1 The rules

15.5 One trade

Risk \(l = \$1{,}750\); stop \(s = m\sigma = 1.07 \times 0.2737\% = 0.2928\%\) of the price; at 31,070.00 that is 91.0 points.

Notional \(N = l/s = \$597{,}582\); \(\$19.23\) per point; lots = this divided by the contract's dollars per point per lot, rounded down.

Cost \(c = \kappa N = \$38.25\), \(\rho = c/l = 2.19\%\); the risk is sized so that \(l(1+\rho)\) fits the room (5.4).

First trade of phase 1: \(w = \min(k\,l,\ A_1,\ \text{caps}) = \$2{,}000\); take-profit \(106.0\) points away; \(p = l/(l+w+c) = 0.4620\).

Mean \(-c\); variance \(l(w+c) = 3{,}566{,}929\); duration about 1.33 h of open market (losers 1.27 h, winners 1.4 h), cut at 20:00 UTC.

A full funded win, with every cap the engine applies (the plan's 40%, the firm's best-day share, day cap or ceiling): \(\$3{,}750\) at risk \(\$1{,}750\): take-profit 197 points away, \(p = 0.3160\).

Margin at the funded leverage 1:10: \(\$59{,}758\) = 59.8% of the starting balance (limit 60%); smallest stop \(m_{\min} = 1.066\).

15.6 The evaluation

Phase 1: \(A = \$5{,}000\), \(B = \$10{,}000\)

Chain (5.5): \(P_1 = 0.6193\), \(\mathbb{E}[\text{trades}] = 15.88\), \(K = \$584\), \(\mathbb{E}[X_\text{end}\mid\text{fail}] = -\$10{,}000\); identity gap +0.0000; textbook \((B-K)/(A+B) = 0.6269\); zero cost, exact landing \(B/(A+B) = 0.6667\).

Rules in play: every win at most $2,000; 4 trading days (filler trades in the engine).

Daily loss limit $4,000: each trade's risk plus cost fits today's room less $200; after 2 full losses in one day the remaining room is smaller than a full trade, so the plan trades smaller or waits until tomorrow.

Phase 2: \(A = \$10{,}000\), \(B = \$10{,}000\)

Chain (5.5): \(P_2 = 0.4706\), \(\mathbb{E}[\text{trades}] = 15.34\), \(K = \$557\), \(\mathbb{E}[X_\text{end}\mid\text{fail}] = -\$10{,}000\); identity gap +0.0000; textbook \((B-K)/(A+B) = 0.4717\); zero cost, exact landing \(B/(A+B) = 0.5000\).

Rules in play: every win at most $4,000; 4 trading days (filler trades in the engine).

Daily loss limit $4,000: each trade's risk plus cost fits today's room less $200; after 2 full losses in one day the remaining room is smaller than a full trade, so the plan trades smaller or waits until tomorrow.

Both phases: \(P = 0.6193 \times 0.4706 = 0.2914\) (chain). Engine with every rule, fresh paths: 0.2875 ± 0.0057.

15.11 How the plan meets the rules

  • Nasdaq with the stop widened to fit 1:10 leverage (USDJPY was searched too)
  • each day's take-profit capped at 25% of the cycle target less $1 per $100K (and 40% per trade), so the best-day rule always holds
  • one Classic account of each size: 100K, 50K, 25K, 10K and 5K
  • flat every day by 20:00 UTC (no entry from 19:00 UTC), so the account is flat through every daily reset and pays no overnight financing; flat at weekends
  • concentration policy: no trade and no day makes more than 40% of the current phase or payout target, so every phase takes at least three winning days
  • at least 10 minutes between a close and the next entry; no take-profit and no stop closer than 0.6 hourly standard deviations to the entry, so fewer than 1 trade in 1,000 can close within 2 minutes
  • risk per trade sized so that the stop loss plus the round-trip cost fits the room under the floor and under the day's limit; when the room above the floor is below a tenth of the normal risk, one last trade with its stop at the floor: the account recovers or ends in a breach that frees its allocation
  • minimum trading days completed, where fillers are allowed, with one minimal trade a day held at least 2 minutes and a stop of at most $3 per $100K

Sources: intercom.help/fxify/en/articles/12130186-2-phase-account-types (refunds by type); intercom.help/fxify/en/articles/14834680-the-2-phase-classic-account; fxify.com/blog/cheapest-path-funded-50k-fxify/ (refund guide, 14 September 2026).

16.11 How the plan meets the rules

  • its own market (no other account trades it), with its own direction rule: its trades are never copies
  • one account at the larger size if it is worth more
  • flat around news
  • flat every day by 20:00 UTC (no entry from 19:00 UTC), so the account is flat through every daily reset and pays no overnight financing; flat at weekends
  • concentration policy: no trade and no day makes more than 40% of the current phase or payout target, so every phase takes at least three winning days
  • at least 10 minutes between a close and the next entry; no take-profit and no stop closer than 0.6 hourly standard deviations to the entry, so fewer than 1 trade in 1,000 can close within 2 minutes
  • risk per trade sized so that the stop loss plus the round-trip cost fits the room under the floor and under the day's limit; when the room above the floor is below a tenth of the normal risk, one last trade with its stop at the floor: the account recovers or ends in a breach that frees its allocation
  • minimum trading days completed, where fillers are allowed, with one minimal trade a day held at least 2 minutes and a stop of at most $3 per $100K

One 100K account; each row's own chosen setting on sixteen fresh paths (121–136), 24,000 attempts, cluster-robust 95% interval. Version 7 reported $3,209 a month for this programme under its rules and engine.

17.5 One trade

Risk \(l = \$1{,}750\); stop \(s = m\sigma = 1 \times 0.2737\% = 0.2737\%\) of the price; at 31,070.00 that is 85.0 points.

Notional \(N = l/s = \$639{,}413\); \(\$20.58\) per point; lots = this divided by the contract's dollars per point per lot, rounded down.

Cost \(c = \kappa N = \$40.92\), \(\rho = c/l = 2.34\%\); the risk is sized so that \(l(1+\rho)\) fits the room (5.4).

First trade of phase 1: \(w = \min(k\,l,\ A_1,\ \text{caps}) = \$3{,}200\); take-profit \(157.5\) points away; \(p = l/(l+w+c) = 0.3506\).

Mean \(-c\); variance \(l(w+c) = 5{,}671{,}614\); duration about 1.85 h of open market (losers 1.57 h, winners 2.4 h), cut at 20:00 UTC.

A full funded win, with every cap the engine applies (the plan's 40%, the firm's best-day share, day cap or ceiling): \(\$8{,}000\) at risk \(\$1{,}750\): take-profit 391 points away, \(p = 0.1787\).

Margin at the funded leverage 1:50: \(\$12{,}788\) = 12.8% of the starting balance (limit 60%); smallest stop \(m_{\min} = 0.213\).

17.6 The evaluation

Phase 1: \(A = \$8{,}000\), \(B = \$10{,}000\)

Chain (5.5): \(P_1 = 0.5168\), \(\mathbb{E}[\text{trades}] = 15.67\), \(K = \$608\), \(\mathbb{E}[X_\text{end}\mid\text{fail}] = -\$10{,}000\); identity gap -0.0000; textbook \((B-K)/(A+B) = 0.5212\); zero cost, exact landing \(B/(A+B) = 0.5556\).

Rules in play: every win at most $3,200; 3 trading days (filler trades in the engine).

Daily loss limit $5,000: each trade's risk plus cost fits today's room less $200; after 2 full losses in one day the remaining room is smaller than a full trade, so the plan trades smaller or waits until tomorrow.

Phase 2: \(A = \$6{,}000\), \(B = \$10{,}000\)

Chain (5.5): \(P_2 = 0.5803\), \(\mathbb{E}[\text{trades}] = 15.69\), \(K = \$614\), \(\mathbb{E}[X_\text{end}\mid\text{fail}] = -\$10{,}000\); identity gap -0.0000; textbook \((B-K)/(A+B) = 0.5859\); zero cost, exact landing \(B/(A+B) = 0.6250\).

Rules in play: every win at most $2,400; 3 trading days (filler trades in the engine).

Daily loss limit $5,000: each trade's risk plus cost fits today's room less $200; after 2 full losses in one day the remaining room is smaller than a full trade, so the plan trades smaller or waits until tomorrow.

Both phases: \(P = 0.5168 \times 0.5803 = 0.2999\) (chain). Engine with every rule, fresh paths: 0.2970 ± 0.0058.

17.11 How the plan meets the rules

  • Nasdaq, shared direction; 5 × 100K: $500,000 is about €444,000 at 1.1256, inside the €500,000 cap, and 100K is cheaper per dollar than 200K
  • risk 1.75%, below the 3% limit
  • every position closed before a payout request
  • flat every day by 20:00 UTC (no entry from 19:00 UTC), so the account is flat through every daily reset and pays no overnight financing; flat at weekends
  • concentration policy: no trade and no day makes more than 40% of the current phase or payout target, so every phase takes at least three winning days
  • at least 10 minutes between a close and the next entry; no take-profit and no stop closer than 0.6 hourly standard deviations to the entry, so fewer than 1 trade in 1,000 can close within 2 minutes
  • risk per trade sized so that the stop loss plus the round-trip cost fits the room under the floor and under the day's limit; when the room above the floor is below a tenth of the normal risk, one last trade with its stop at the floor: the account recovers or ends in a breach that frees its allocation
  • minimum trading days completed, where fillers are allowed, with one minimal trade a day held at least 2 minutes and a stop of at most $3 per $100K

Sources: https://support.fintokei.com/en/articles/6538826-how-are-the-daily-loss-limit-and-maximum-loss-limit-calculated; support.fintokei.com/en/articles/6538884 (withdrawals, minimum amount); support.fintokei.com/en/articles/8428030 (minimum trading days); https://support.fintokei.com/en/articles/9464503-what-is-all-in-trading-and-why-is-it-forbidden-to-achieve-profit-target-in-one-trade.

Chain (5.5): \(P_1 = 0.4571\), \(\mathbb{E}[\text{trades}] = 15.39\), \(K = \$794\), \(\mathbb{E}[X_\text{end}\mid\text{fail}] = -\$10{,}000\); identity gap +0.0000; textbook \((B-K)/(A+B) = 0.4599\); zero cost, exact landing \(B/(A+B) = 0.5000\).

Rules in play: every win at most $4,000; 3 trading days (filler trades in the engine).

Daily loss limit $5,000: each trade's risk plus cost fits today's room less $200; after 2 full losses in one day the remaining room is smaller than a full trade, so the plan trades smaller or waits until tomorrow.

Phase 2: \(A = \$5{,}000\), \(B = \$10{,}000\)

Chain (5.5): \(P_2 = 0.5884\), \(\mathbb{E}[\text{trades}] = 16.64\), \(K = \$872\), \(\mathbb{E}[X_\text{end}\mid\text{fail}] = -\$10{,}000\); identity gap +0.0000; textbook \((B-K)/(A+B) = 0.6077\); zero cost, exact landing \(B/(A+B) = 0.6667\).

Rules in play: every win at most $2,000; 3 trading days (filler trades in the engine).

Daily loss limit $5,000: each trade's risk plus cost fits today's room less $200; after 2 full losses in one day the remaining room is smaller than a full trade, so the plan trades smaller or waits until tomorrow.

Both phases: \(P = 0.4571 \times 0.5884 = 0.2689\) (chain). Engine with every rule, fresh paths: 0.2692 ± 0.0056.

19.11 How the plan meets the rules

  • Nasdaq, 1.75% risk (below the 2% rule after cost)
  • bi-weekly payouts; until the funded account has 5 trading days, at most one normal trade a day
  • trade durations recorded: the plan's trades last hours on average
  • flat every day by 20:00 UTC (no entry from 19:00 UTC), so the account is flat through every daily reset and pays no overnight financing; flat at weekends
  • concentration policy: no trade and no day makes more than 40% of the current phase or payout target, so every phase takes at least three winning days
  • at least 10 minutes between a close and the next entry; no take-profit and no stop closer than 0.6 hourly standard deviations to the entry, so fewer than 1 trade in 1,000 can close within 2 minutes
  • risk per trade sized so that the stop loss plus the round-trip cost fits the room under the floor and under the day's limit; when the room above the floor is below a tenth of the normal risk, one last trade with its stop at the floor: the account recovers or ends in a breach that frees its allocation
  • minimum trading days completed, where fillers are allowed, with one minimal trade a day held at least 2 minutes and a stop of at most $3 per $100K

Sources: help.alphacapitalgroup.uk/en/articles/10570531-bi-weekly-performance-fee; https://help.alphacapitalgroup.uk/en/articles/8447268-what-is-the-2-minute-average-trade-duration-rule; help.alphacapitalgroup.uk/en/articles/8420429-alpha-pro-8-10.

20.5 One trade

Risk \(l = \$1{,}750\); stop \(s = m\sigma = 1.07 \times 0.2737\% = 0.2928\%\) of the price; at 31,070.00 that is 91.0 points.

Notional \(N = l/s = \$597{,}582\); \(\$19.23\) per point; lots = this divided by the contract's dollars per point per lot, rounded down.

Cost \(c = \kappa N = \$38.25\), \(\rho = c/l = 2.19\%\); the risk is sized so that \(l(1+\rho)\) fits the room (5.4).

First trade of phase 1: \(w = \min(k\,l,\ A_1,\ \text{caps}) = \$4{,}000\); take-profit \(210.0\) points away; \(p = l/(l+w+c) = 0.3023\).

Mean \(-c\); variance \(l(w+c) = 7{,}066{,}929\); duration about 2.64 h of open market (losers 2.14 h, winners 3.8 h), cut at 20:00 UTC.

A full funded win, with every cap the engine applies (the plan's 40%, the firm's best-day share, day cap or ceiling): \(\$12{,}000\) at risk \(\$1{,}750\): take-profit 626 points away, \(p = 0.1269\).

Margin at the funded leverage 1:30: \(\$19{,}919\) = 19.9% of the starting balance (limit 20%); smallest stop \(m_{\min} = 1.066\).

Value per month (8.3): \(30.44 \times 1{,}568/21.73 = \)\(\$2{,}197\) ± $135 (95%, cluster-robust) per account on sixteen fresh paths.

21.11 How the plan meets the rules

  • one account; Nasdaq with a stop of at least 1.066 sd at 1.75% risk (funded 1:10, margin at most 60%) or USDJPY
  • take-profit capped at the day's remaining $3,000
  • one open position at a time, risking at most 1.75% plus cost: Goat Guard's 2% cannot trigger without an intraday gap
  • flat every day by 20:00 UTC (no entry from 19:00 UTC), so the account is flat through every daily reset and pays no overnight financing; flat at weekends
  • concentration policy: no trade and no day makes more than 40% of the current phase or payout target, so every phase takes at least three winning days
  • at least 10 minutes between a close and the next entry; no take-profit and no stop closer than 0.6 hourly standard deviations to the entry, so fewer than 1 trade in 1,000 can close within 2 minutes
  • risk per trade sized so that the stop loss plus the round-trip cost fits the room under the floor and under the day's limit; when the room above the floor is below a tenth of the normal risk, one last trade with its stop at the floor: the account recovers or ends in a breach that frees its allocation
  • minimum trading days completed, where fillers are allowed, with one minimal trade a day held at least 2 minutes and a stop of at most $3 per $100K

Sources: help.goatfundedtrader.com/en/articles/13575169-2-step-standard; help.goatfundedtrader.com/en/articles/9549359-all-about-rewards-and-profit-splits; https://help.goatfundedtrader.com/en/articles/10742107-what-is-goat-guard.

One 100K account; each row's own chosen setting on sixteen fresh paths (121–136), 24,000 attempts, cluster-robust 95% interval. Version 7 reported $2,766 a month for this programme under its rules and engine.

22.5 One trade

Risk \(l = \$1{,}750\); stop \(s = m\sigma = 1 \times 0.2737\% = 0.2737\%\) of the price; at 31,070.00 that is 85.0 points.

Notional \(N = l/s = \$639{,}413\); \(\$20.58\) per point; lots = this divided by the contract's dollars per point per lot, rounded down.

Cost \(c = \kappa N = \$40.92\), \(\rho = c/l = 2.34\%\); the risk is sized so that \(l(1+\rho)\) fits the room (5.4).

First trade of phase 1: \(w = \min(k\,l,\ A_1,\ \text{caps}) = \$4{,}000\); take-profit \(196.4\) points away; \(p = l/(l+w+c) = 0.3022\).

Mean \(-c\); variance \(l(w+c) = 7{,}071{,}614\); duration about 2.31 h of open market (losers 1.87 h, winners 3.3 h), cut at 20:00 UTC.

A full funded win, with every cap the engine applies (the plan's 40%, the firm's best-day share, day cap or ceiling): \(\$12{,}000\) at risk \(\$1{,}750\): take-profit 585 points away, \(p = 0.1269\).

Margin at the funded leverage 1:20: \(\$31{,}971\) = 32.0% of the starting balance (limit 60%); smallest stop \(m_{\min} = 0.533\).

22.6 The evaluation

Phase 1: \(A = \$10{,}000\), \(B = \$10{,}000\)

Chain (5.5): \(P_1 = 0.4684\), \(\mathbb{E}[\text{trades}] = 15.35\), \(K = \$595\), \(\mathbb{E}[X_\text{end}\mid\text{fail}] = -\$10{,}000\); identity gap +0.0000; textbook \((B-K)/(A+B) = 0.4698\); zero cost, exact landing \(B/(A+B) = 0.5000\).

Rules in play: every win at most $4,000; 5 trading days (filler trades in the engine).

Daily loss limit $5,000: each trade's risk plus cost fits today's room less $200; after 2 full losses in one day the remaining room is smaller than a full trade, so the plan trades smaller or waits until tomorrow.

Phase 2: \(A = \$5{,}000\), \(B = \$10{,}000\)

Chain (5.5): \(P_2 = 0.6154\), \(\mathbb{E}[\text{trades}] = 15.95\), \(K = \$628\), \(\mathbb{E}[X_\text{end}\mid\text{fail}] = -\$10{,}000\); identity gap +0.0000; textbook \((B-K)/(A+B) = 0.6240\); zero cost, exact landing \(B/(A+B) = 0.6667\).

Rules in play: every win at most $2,000; 5 trading days (filler trades in the engine).

Daily loss limit $5,000: each trade's risk plus cost fits today's room less $200; after 2 full losses in one day the remaining room is smaller than a full trade, so the plan trades smaller or waits until tomorrow.

Both phases: \(P = 0.4684 \times 0.6154 = 0.2882\) (chain). Engine with every rule, fresh paths: 0.2940 ± 0.0058.

24.5 One trade

Risk \(l = \$1{,}500\); stop \(s = m\sigma = 1 \times 0.2737\% = 0.2737\%\) of the price; at 31,070.00 that is 85.0 points.

Notional \(N = l/s = \$548{,}068\); \(\$17.64\) per point; lots = this divided by the contract's dollars per point per lot, rounded down.

Cost \(c = \kappa N = \$35.08\), \(\rho = c/l = 2.34\%\); the risk is sized so that \(l(1+\rho)\) fits the room (5.4).

First trade of phase 1: \(w = \min(k\,l,\ A_1,\ \text{caps}) = \$2{,}667\); take-profit \(153.2\) points away; \(p = l/(l+w+c) = 0.3570\).

Mean \(-c\); variance \(l(w+c) = 4{,}052{,}615\); duration about 1.80 h of open market (losers 1.53 h, winners 2.3 h), cut at 20:00 UTC.

A full funded win, with every cap the engine applies (the plan's 40%, the firm's best-day share, day cap or ceiling): \(\$1{,}960\) at risk \(\$1{,}307\) (lowered from \(\$1{,}500\) so that the reward:risk is at least 1:1.5): take-profit 130 points away, \(p = 0.3963\).

Margin at the funded leverage 1:20: \(\$27{,}403\) = 27.4% of the starting balance (limit 60%); smallest stop \(m_{\min} = 0.457\).

24.6 The evaluation

Phase 1: \(A = \$8{,}000\), \(B = \$8{,}000\)

Chain (5.5): \(P_1 = 0.4697\), \(\mathbb{E}[\text{trades}] = 15.01\), \(K = \$475\), \(\mathbb{E}[X_\text{end}\mid\text{fail}] = -\$8{,}000\); identity gap -0.0000; textbook \((B-K)/(A+B) = 0.4703\); zero cost, exact landing \(B/(A+B) = 0.5000\).

Rules in play: every win at most $3,200; 3 profitable days needed: the engine aims at a daily target (6.4), which the chain leaves out.

Daily loss limit $4,000: each trade's risk plus cost fits today's room less $200; after 2 full losses in one day the remaining room is smaller than a full trade, so the plan trades smaller or waits until tomorrow.

Phase 2: \(A = \$5{,}000\), \(B = \$8{,}000\)

Chain (5.5): \(P_2 = 0.5742\), \(\mathbb{E}[\text{trades}] = 16.90\), \(K = \$483\), \(\mathbb{E}[X_\text{end}\mid\text{fail}] = -\$8{,}000\); identity gap -0.0000; textbook \((B-K)/(A+B) = 0.5782\); zero cost, exact landing \(B/(A+B) = 0.6154\).

Rules in play: every win at most $2,000; 3 profitable days needed: the engine aims at a daily target (6.4), which the chain leaves out.

Daily loss limit $4,000: each trade's risk plus cost fits today's room less $200; after 2 full losses in one day the remaining room is smaller than a full trade, so the plan trades smaller or waits until tomorrow.

Both phases: \(P = 0.4697 \times 0.5742 = 0.2697\) (chain). Engine with every rule, fresh paths: 0.2632 ± 0.0056.

24.11 How the plan meets the rules

  • one 100K account, Nasdaq
  • every trade at a reward:risk of at least 1:1.5
  • funded profit never above $4,900 (payout targets of 2–4.9%), $100 under the threshold, so the 50% rule never applies even with a take-profit filled a few points better
  • no win placed that could take the profit closed in any 30 days above $10,000
  • the risk interview booked as soon as it is offered
  • flat every day by 20:00 UTC (no entry from 19:00 UTC), so the account is flat through every daily reset and pays no overnight financing; flat at weekends
  • concentration policy: no trade and no day makes more than 40% of the current phase or payout target, so every phase takes at least three winning days
  • at least 10 minutes between a close and the next entry; no take-profit and no stop closer than 0.6 hourly standard deviations to the entry, so fewer than 1 trade in 1,000 can close within 2 minutes
  • risk per trade sized so that the stop loss plus the round-trip cost fits the room under the floor and under the day's limit; when the room above the floor is below a tenth of the normal risk, one last trade with its stop at the floor: the account recovers or ends in a breach that frees its allocation
  • minimum trading days completed, where fillers are allowed, with one minimal trade a day held at least 2 minutes and a stop of at most $3 per $100K

Sources: https://maventrading.com/faqs (read 4 Oct 2026).

Part III

One person, many accounts

Part II valued one account at a time, as a long-run rate. This part runs them together for one person: how much each firm allows, how to fill it, what a first year looks like, what the long run is, how much cash it takes, and what happens if costs are higher, markets move together, news must be avoided, or payouts are refused.

27.2 Four 100K accounts or two 200K?

Scale every dollar amount of a programme by \(\lambda\) (account, targets, limits, risk, payout target, safety margins). The engine's rules then scale exactly: every trade's win, loss and cost scale by \(\lambda\), the pass probabilities and the time are unchanged, and the funded account's trading cash scales by \(\lambda\). The fee need not scale, and the refund follows the fee actually paid. Writing \(G(S)\) for the funded cash excluding the refund, \(R(S)\) for the refund and \(q_R\) for the chance of reaching the payout that carries it (given a pass):

Scaling an account (rules that scale) $$\mathbb{E}[V(\lambda S)] = P\big[\lambda\, G(S) + q_R\, R(\lambda S)\big] - F(\lambda S), \qquad \mathbb{E}[T](\lambda S) = \mathbb{E}[T](S).$$

With \(R = F\), one account of size \(\lambda S\) is worth more than \(\lambda\) accounts of size \(S\) by \(\big[\lambda F(S) - F(\lambda S)\big](1 - P\,q_R)\) per attempt: the fee saving, less the part of it that would have come back as a refund. (Version 6's formula scaled the refund with the account; it does not.) The formula does not apply where a rule is a fixed dollar amount: GFT's $3,000 day cap and $10,000 payout cap, Maven's $5,000 threshold (the plan's $4,900 ceiling) and $10,000 rolling cap, the minimum payouts ($50 to $150), and whole futures contracts. Chapter 11 checks the scaling path by path on the same seeds, once with the 200K fee set to twice the 100K fee (the rules) and once with the real fee (the contracts).

Programme100K fee200K fee200K − 2 × 100K fee2 × 100K: EV/month1 × 200K: EV/monthDifference
FTMO 2-Step$607.82$1,215.65$0.00$5,024$5,024+$0
FundedNext Stellar$549.99$1,049.99−$49.99$2,778$2,841+$63
Fintokei ProTrader$549.00$1,249.00$151.00$5,754$5,560−$195
Alpha Capital Pro 10%$497.00$997.00$3.00$5,306$5,278−$28
FunderPro Classic$539.00$989.00−$89.00$5,838$5,976+$138
GFT Standard$524.00$974.00−$74.00$4,395$3,558−$836
BrightFunded Classic$559.42$1,122.22$3.38$5,213$5,208−$5
Blue Guardian 2-Step$579.00$1,162.00$4.00$4,463$4,469+$6

Same setting scaled, same sixteen paths and random numbers. Where every rule scales with the account, the only differences are the fee and the refund that follows it (11.1 checks the scaling path by path); GFT's flat $3,000 day cap and $10,000 payout cap do not scale.

The answer, firm by firm. With the corrected formula of 27.2, 200K beats 2 × 100K exactly when \(F(2S) < 2F(S)\), by \((2F(S) - F(2S))(1 - P q_R)\) per attempt, unless a rule fails to scale. Where the plan holds one account (FunderPro, whose $200,000 cap allows one 200K; GFT and FundedNext, which forbid duplicated trades between their own funded accounts, so a second account would need a market no other account of the firm trades), the larger size is used if it is worth more than one smaller account. GFT is the case where the formula fails: its $3,000 day cap and $10,000 payout cap bind harder at 200K, so one 200K account is worth less than two 100K accounts would be; two GFT accounts on the same trades are not allowed, and one 200K account is still worth more than one 100K account.

FTMO, 4 × 100K or 2 × 200K. Same fee per dollar, same rules: the paired difference on the same 1,200 seeds is −$0 ± $0 a month for the whole portfolio. In the model the four 100K accounts copy the same trades, so their cash is exactly twice that of two 200K accounts up to rounding: neither is smoother. Two accounts make half as many payout requests, which matters only if refusals are a per-request risk (Chapter 29). The plan uses four 100K accounts for the headline.

Chapter 28

Running every account at once

28.1 What adds up and what does not

By linearity (2.1), the expected cash of a set of slots over a horizon is the sum of their expected cash over that horizon, under the policy actually run, however correlated the accounts are. Correlation changes the spread: in a bad week every Nasdaq account loses together, fees fall due together, and a finite budget can run short together.

28.2 One direction per market

No two of your accounts ever hold opposite positions on one market at the same time. When a new trade opens and another of your accounts already holds that market, the new trade takes the same direction; when none does, the plan's rule decides (the sign of the last five hourly closes). The rule has no edge (Chapter 11); its job is to keep every account on one side.

28.3 The lockstep simulation

The portfolio results come from an engine that runs every account of one person on shared price paths and one calendar (lockstep.py), processing trades in the order of their entry time so the shared-direction rule is exact, applying every firm's rules (with the plan's policies) to its own accounts, and writing every fee, credit, phase result and payout to a dated ledger. The settings were frozen before any portfolio run, and each simulated life runs on its own new paths (path numbers 100,000 and up, each market its own stream, 3.3), used by nothing else; version 7's market offsets made one life's yen path another life's Nasdaq path, which version 8 removes. Lives are therefore independent and their average is an unbiased estimate with an ordinary interval. In 8,400 simulated years, no two accounts held opposite positions.

1,200 simulated first years per portfolio, each on its own new price paths (seeds from 100,000; the same seeds for every portfolio, so differences are paired). First year: total cash over 12 months ÷ 12, after every fee modelled. Sum of long-run rates: the accounts' renewal–reward values from Part II, a different quantity (8.5). Lowest cumulative cash: the deepest point of fees paid minus cash received.

Three quantities. The recommended portfolio's first year averages $62,198 a month (±$2,116); months 2–12 average $70,488; the sum of its accounts' long-run rates is $74,751. They answer different questions (8.5), and Section 28.6 measures the long-run rate of the portfolio itself. 98.0% of simulated first years ended ahead; the 5th percentile of first-year cash is $96,538.

No account ever opposed another. In 8,400 simulated years the engine recorded 0 moments when two accounts held opposite positions on one market. The recommended plan places about 67 orders a trading day across all accounts.

Paired differences (same seeds, 95%): Full allowance (each firm at the largest allocation modelled) minus Recommended (A + B + one account per tier-D firm): +$9,715 ± $525; Full allowance + The5ers' small New accounts minus Full allowance (each firm at the largest allocation modelled): +$1,613 ± $655; Recommended + futures layer minus Recommended (A + B + one account per tier-D firm): +$6,025 ± $828; Tiers A and B minus Tier A only: +$16,265 ± $884; Recommended (A + B + one account per tier-D firm) minus Tiers A and B: +$20,627 ± $1,324.

28.6 The long run, and what is left at month 12

Period (36-month lives)Average cash per month (95%)
months 1–12$63,862 ± $2,597
months 13–24$73,531 ± $2,814
months 25–36$72,784 ± $2,742
month 12 alone$76,236 ± $7,749
months 11 and 13$78,137 and $78,991

800 simulated 36-month lives of the recommended plan, each on its own new paths (path numbers 300,000 and up).

Months 13–36. They average $73,157 ± $1,974 a month. That is a finite-window estimate, not by itself the asymptotic rate. Its two annual windows differ by +$747 ± $3,911 a month (months 13–24 minus 25–36, paired over the same lives, 95%): no measurable trend, so the window average is read as an estimate of the long-run rate. The sum of the accounts' long-run rates from Part II is $74,751 ± $2,063; the window average is $1,594 lower (1.1 standard errors). The first year is lower ($63,862) because it carries the start-up: fees at once, payouts later. Value of the accounts open at month 12: the cash those accounts (evaluations in progress and funded accounts) go on to pay after month 12 averages $168,447 (±$12,509); it belongs to the first year's purchases but arrives later.

Each firm alone, on the same paths. On the first 200 of these lives, every firm's accounts were also run alone, on the same paths and calendar. This is a comparison of two policies, not a test of linearity: alone, a firm's account chooses its own direction where in the portfolio it copies the direction of an account already open, so the trades differ. Full portfolio minus the sum of the firms alone: −$1,700 ± $2,566 a month over months 13–36 and +$595 ± $3,297 in the first year (paired, 95%). Running the accounts together makes no measurable difference here. On those same paths the firms alone average $75,847 a month, against the sum of their long-run rates in Part II of $74,751: every account on the Nasdaq trades the same path, largely in the same direction, so their sampling errors move together and a sample of a few hundred lives can sit some thousands of dollars from the expectation.

28.7 Finite cash, higher costs, correlation, news and behaviour

A finite budget. The first-year figures assume every slot is refilled the moment its attempt ends, which needs cash for fees before payouts arrive. Version 6 quoted the median of the deepest cash shortfall as if it kept every slot filled; half of all years need more. Here the plan is run with a fixed starting budget instead: a purchase waits until the cash is there.

Chapter 29

If payouts can be refused

Every firm keeps the right to refuse a payout it judges to break its rules. Nobody publishes how often it happens. This chapter turns that uncertainty into scenarios; it does not cover payouts lost because a firm judges the strategy itself to break a behavioural rule (Chapter 31.2), which would not be random.

29.1 A model

Suppose each payout request at a firm is refused with probability \(\phi\), independently, and a refusal ends your use of that firm. The number of paid requests before the first refusal is geometric, with mean \((1-\phi)/\phi\). If the firm's requests arrive as a Poisson process at rate \(r\) a month (an assumption made for this illustration only), each of gross size \(a\), the expected cash actually paid over \(H\) months is

Paid cash under refusals $$a\,r\,(1-\phi)\,\frac{1 - e^{-\phi r H}}{\phi r} \;=\; a\,r\,H \times (1-\phi)\,\frac{1 - e^{-\phi r H}}{\phi r H},$$

the all-paid cash \(arH\) times the average survival fraction and the factor \((1-\phi)\): the refused request itself is not paid. For \(n\) requests on a fixed schedule instead, the expected paid amount is \(a\,(1-\phi)\,\big[1-(1-\phi)^n\big]/\phi\). Neither formula is used for the figures below: the simulation applies the refusals to each life's actual dated payout requests. Fees paid while the firm is still used count against the paid cash. In the recommended plan the FTMO accounts together make about 1.00 requests a month: at \(\phi = 2\%\), FTMO is expected to last \(1/(0.02 \times 1.00) = 50\) months.

Part IV

Running it

The settings of every account in one place, the routine that runs them, and everything that could make the numbers wrong.

The routine

Stops in points (Nasdaq), pips (USDJPY, EURUSD) or dollars (gold) at the prices of 2 October 2026; recompute \(m\sigma\times\)price weekly and re-measure \(\sigma\) monthly. The take-profit is the smallest of \(k\) times the risk, the distance to the current target, 40% of the current target less today's profit, and any firm cap, plus the cost (4.1).

30.2 Placing a trade, step by step

  1. Is a trade allowed now? Weekdays, inside the market's entry hours and not from 19:00 UTC onward; not before 00:05 UTC (after the midnight resets); no restricted news window for this firm, stage and market coming up before the trade could reasonably end (use the firm's own calendar); at least 10 minutes since this account's last close, whatever its result and direction (Hola Prime groups any re-entry within 10 minutes; FundingPips groups trade ideas).
  2. Direction. If any of your accounts holds this market, the same direction. Otherwise the plan's rule: long if the last hourly close is above the close five hours earlier, short if below. The rule has no edge (Chapter 11); its job is that no two of your accounts are ever opposite.
  3. Room. Floor room \(= (\text{balance} - \text{floor} - \$100 \text{ per } \$100\text{K})/(1+\rho)\); day room \(= (\text{daily limit} - \text{today's loss} - \$200 \text{ per } \$100\text{K})/(1+\rho)\); margin room \(= 0.6\,\lambda\, m\sigma \times \text{balance}\). If the floor room is under a tenth of the normal risk, trade the rest: one bracket whose stop sits at the floor itself (risk = balance − floor, net of cost, if the day room allows it, else the day room; one contract on futures). It either recovers or ends the account in a breach, which frees its allocation; buy the replacement only then. If only the day room is short, wait until tomorrow.
  4. Risk. \(l\) = the smallest of the plan's risk and the three rooms. Stop: \(m\sigma\times\) price; size \(= l /\) (stop in points \(\times\) dollars per point per lot), rounded down (whole contracts on futures, never fewer than one: if one contract at this stop risks more than \(l\), trade one contract if the floor and day rooms allow it).
  5. Take-profit. \(w\) = the smaller of \(k\,l\) and the distance to the current target (or today's profitable-day target), but never a take-profit closer than 0.6 hourly standard deviations (finishing a little above a target is fine); then capped by 40% of the current target less today's profit (the whole calendar day's, including any made before a payout request today) and any firm cap (FXIFY 25% of the payout target per day less $1 per $100K, GFT's $3,000 a day less today's profit, Maven: the distance to $4,900 of funded profit less $550 per $100K for each profitable day the cycle still needs after today, and for today as well if this win would leave today short of +0.5%, and the distance to $10,000 of closed profit in any 30 days). If a cap leaves less than the minimum distance: with nothing made today, lower the risk until the cap is a full minimum-distance win; otherwise no further trade today. At Maven, if \(w < 1.5\,l\), lower \(l\) to \(w/1.5\). Distance = stop distance \(\times (w + c)/l\).
  6. Place one bracket order; never move it. At 20:00 UTC close everything still open.
  7. Log it: time, account, market, direction, size, stop, target, fill, and the closing reason. The log is the evidence for any review.

30.3 Phases, payouts and refills

  • When a phase target (plus $20 per $100K) is reached: stop. Where the firm counts trading days, place one minimal trade, held at least 2 minutes with a stop at most $3 per $100K away, on each of the next trading days not yet traded (at most five, so they cannot lose the $20); where it counts profitable days, the daily target of 6.4 has met it.
  • When a funded account reaches its payout target: stop, request the payout on the first allowed day (counted from the account's first trade), withdraw everything above the start, and start the next cycle. At Alpha Capital the first request also needs five trading days: until then trade at most once a day, past the target if needed. At Fintokei every request needs three trading days in the cycle (fillers allowed).
  • When a payout date arrives before the target is reached, request whatever profit the account holds, if the firm's minimum (FundingPips 1% of the account; FXIFY $50; Fintokei, GFT, FunderPro, Blue Guardian, Alpha $100; The5ers $150) and its consistency and day rules allow it (FXIFY: no day above 25% of it; profitable days at FundingPips 95%, GFT, Hola Prime, Maven, Blue Guardian), and start the next cycle; if a rule does not allow it, keep trading toward a target large enough for the rule to hold.
  • When an account fails (a breach, including the last trade of step 3): buy the next evaluation of the same programme (at FundedNext, after a pause if its gambling indicators are a concern: 28.7). Every account ends in a breach or in normal trading; none is left open and unused.
  • Credits (The5ers) are spent on the next evaluation at that firm.

30.4 Cash needed

Fees are paid before payouts arrive. In the simulation the running total of the recommended plan falls to −$55,519 at its lowest in a typical first year, −$136,974 in the worst 5% and −$210,180 in the worst 1%. These are not amounts that keep every slot filled in every year: Section 28.7 runs the plan with fixed budgets, purchases waiting for cash, and shows what each budget gives.

30.5 Workload

About 67 bracket orders a trading day across 28 accounts, a daily close-out at 20:00 UTC, payout requests and evaluation purchases.

Chapter 31

What could make the numbers wrong

31.1 Higher trading costs

FunderPro ClassicNasdaq$2,671$2,370$2,25018.517.4
